A racial distribution map of San Francisco, 2010 U.S. Census. Each dot is 25 people: ⬤ White ⬤ Black ⬤ Asian ⬤ Hispanic ⬤ Other

In a 2018 study by the Jewish Community Federation of San Francisco, Jews make up 10% (80,000) of the city's population, making Judaism the second-largest religion in San Francisco after Christianity.[^cite_note-ToI-168] In a 2014 study by the [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center), the largest religious groupings in San Francisco's [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco%E2%80%93Oakland%E2%80%93Hayward,_CA_Metropolitan_Statistical_Area) are Christians (48%), followed by those of [no religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion) (35%), Hindus (5%), Jews (3%), Buddhists (2%), Muslims (1%) and a variety of other religions have smaller followings. In 2014, about 20% of residents in the area are [Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism), and 25% professing [Roman Catholic](606848.md) beliefs. 10% of the residents in metropolitan San Francisco identify as [agnostics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnostics), while 5% identify as [atheists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheism).[^cite_note-173][^cite_note-174]

San Francisco's official city-designated languages[^cite_note-175] are [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_English), [Spanish](26825.md), [Cantonese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantonese_language), [Mandarin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandarin_Chinese), [Filipino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipino_language), and [Vietnamese](32511.md).[^cite_note-176]

In 2010, 55% (411,728) of San Francisco residents spoke only English at home, while 19% (140,302) spoke a [variety of Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Variety_of_Chinese) (mostly [Taishanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taishanese) and [Cantonese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantonese)[^cite_note-177][^cite_note-178]), 12% (88,147) Spanish, 3% (25,767) [Tagalog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tagalog_language), and 2% (14,017) Russian. In total, 45% (342,693) of San Francisco's population spoke a language at home other than English.[^cite_note-179]

#### Ethnic clustering

San Francisco has several prominent Chinese, Mexican, and Filipino neighborhoods including [Chinatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown,_San_Francisco) and [the Mission District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mission_District,_San_Francisco). In 2017, more than half of the Asian population in San Francisco was either Chinese-born (40.3%) or Philippine-born (13.1%). Of the Mexican population, 21% were Mexican-born.[^cite_note-\:02-180] Between 1990 and 2000, the number of foreign-born residents increased from 33% to nearly 40%.[^cite_note-\:02-180] During this same period, the San Francisco metropolitan area received 850,000 immigrants, ranking third in the United States after Los Angeles and New York.[^cite_note-\:02-180]

### Education, households, and income



[Sea Cliff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_Cliff,_San_Francisco) is one of the city's most expensive neighborhoods.

[^cite_note-181]

Of all major cities in the United States, San Francisco has the second-highest percentage of residents with a college degree, second only to [Seattle](11388236.md). Over 44% of adults have a bachelor's or higher degree.[^cite_note-Bizjournals-182] San Francisco had the highest rate at 7,031 per square mile, or over 344,000 total graduates in the city's 46.7 square miles (121 km<sup>2</sup>).[^cite_note-USA_Today-183]

San Francisco has the highest estimated percentage of gay and lesbian individuals of any of the 50 largest U.S. cities, at 15%.[^cite_note-184] San Francisco also has the highest percentage of same-sex households of any American county, with the Bay Area having a higher concentration than any other [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_area).[^cite_note-gaystats-185]

San Francisco ranks third of American cities in median household income[^cite_note-Census_ACS_2003-186] with a 2007 value of \$65,519.[^cite_note-SF_QuickFacts-187] Median family income is \$81,136.[^cite_note-SF_QuickFacts-187] An emigration of middle-class families has left the city with a lower proportion of children than any other large American city,[^cite_note-188] with the dog population cited as exceeding the child population of 115,000, in 2018.[^cite_note-189] In 2007, San Francisco's [poverty rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_rate) was 12%, lower than the national average.[^cite_note-190] [Homelessness has been a chronic problem for San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homelessness_in_San_Francisco) since the early 1970s.[^cite_note-191] The city is believed to have the highest number of homeless inhabitants per capita of any major U.S. city.[^cite_note-192][^cite_note-193]

There are 345,811 households in the city. 133,366 households (38.6%) were individuals, 109,437 (31.6%) were [opposite-sex married couples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marriage), 63,577 (18.4%) had children under the age of 18 living in them, 21,677 (6.3%) were [unmarried opposite-sex partnerships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/POSSLQ), and 10,384 (3.0%) were [same-sex married couples or partnerships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_partnerships). The average household size was 2.26. The average family size was 3.11. 452,986 people (56.3%) lived in rental housing units. 327,985 people (40.7%) lived in owner-occupied housing units. The median age is 38 years.

San Francisco declared itself a [sanctuary city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanctuary_city) in 1989. City officials strengthened the stance in 2013 with its 'Due Process for All' ordinance. The law declared local authorities could not hold immigrants for immigration offenses if they had no violent felonies on their records and did not currently face charges.[^cite_note-194] The city issues a [Resident ID Card](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SF_City_ID_Card) regardless of the applicant's immigration status.[^cite_note-195]

### Homelessness

See also: [Homelessness in the San Francisco Bay Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homelessness_in_the_San_Francisco_Bay_Area)



A [homeless encampment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homelessness_in_the_San_Francisco_Bay_Area) under a freeway in San Francisco

[Homelessness in San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homelessness_in_San_Francisco) emerged as a major issue in the late 20th century and remains a growing problem in modern times.[^cite_note-196]

In 2019, 8,035 homeless people were counted in San Francisco's point-in-time street and shelter count. This was an increase of more than 17% over the 2017 count of 6,858 people. 5,180 of the people were living unsheltered on the streets and in parks.[^cite_note-\:6-197] 26% of respondents in the 2019 count identified job loss as the primary cause of their homelessness, 18% cited alcohol or drug use, and 13% cited being evicted from their residence.[^cite_note-\:6-197]

The city of San Francisco has been dramatically increasing its spending to service the growing population homelessness crisis: spending jumped by \$241 million in 2016–17 to total \$275 million, compared to a budget of just \$34 million the previous year. In 2017–18 the budget for combatting homelessness stood at \$305 million.[^cite_note-Amy_Graff-198] In the 2019–2020 budget year, the city budgeted \$368 million for homelessness services. In the proposed 2020–2021 budget, the city budgeted \$850 million for homelessness services.[^cite_note-199]

In January 2018, a United Nations special rapporteur on homelessness, Leilani Farha, stated that she was "completely shocked" by San Francisco's homelessness crisis during a visit to the city. She compared the "deplorable conditions" of the homeless camps she witnessed on San Francisco's streets to those she had seen in [Mumbai](19189.md).[^cite_note-Amy_Graff-198] In May 2020, San Francisco officially sanctioned [homeless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homelessness_in_the_United_States) [encampments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tent_cities_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-200]

In 2024, according to San Francisco's Point-in-Time (PIT) count, the homeless population was approximately 8,323 individuals. Additionally, over 20,000 people in the city were seeking homeless services.[^cite_note-201] In May 2026, [Daniel Lurie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Lurie) shared that the city's latest Point-in-Time count shows unsheltered homelessness at its lowest level in 15 years.[^cite_note-202][^cite_note-203]

### Crime

Main article: [Crime in San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_in_San_Francisco)

|  | This section needs to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(March 2024)* |
| --- | --- |



[SFPD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SFPD) mounted police officers

San Francisco's violent crime rate is low compared to other major cities.

In 2011, 50 murders were reported, which is 6.1 per 100,000 people.[^cite_note-204] There were about 134 rapes, 3,142 robberies, and about 2,139 assaults. There were about 4,469 burglaries, 25,100 thefts, and 4,210 motor vehicle thefts.[^cite_note-205] The [Tenderloin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tenderloin,_San_Francisco) area has the highest crime rate in San Francisco: 70% of the city's violent crimes, and around one-fourth of the city's murders, occur in this neighborhood. The Tenderloin also sees high rates of drug abuse, gang violence, and prostitution.[^cite_note-206]

Another area with high crime rates is the [Bayview-Hunters Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayview%E2%80%93Hunters_Point,_San_Francisco) area. In the first six months of 2015 there were 25 murders, compared to 14 in the first six months of 2014. However, the murder rate is still much lower than in past decades.[^cite_note-207] That rate rose again by the close of 2016. According to the San Francisco Police Department, there were 59 murders in the city in 2016, an annual total that marked a 13.5% increase in the number of homicides (52) from 2015.[^cite_note-208] The city has also gained a reputation for car break-ins, with over 19,000 car break-ins occurring in 2021.[^cite_note-209]

During the first half of 2018, human feces on San Francisco sidewalks were the second-most-frequent complaint of city residents, with about 65 calls per day. The city has formed a "poop patrol" to attempt to combat the problem.[^cite_note-sfchroniclepoop-210]



SFPD [parking enforcement officers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parking_enforcement_officer)

Several [street gangs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Street_gang) have operated in the city over the decades, including [MS-13](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MS-13),[^cite_note-211] the [Sureños](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sure%C3%B1os) and [Norteños](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norte%C3%B1os) in the Mission District.[^cite_note-212]

African-American street gangs familiar in other cities, including the [Bloods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloods_gang), [Crips](3434023.md), and their sets, have struggled to establish footholds in San Francisco,[^cite_note-213] while police and prosecutors have been accused of liberally labeling young African-American males as gang members.[^cite_note-214]

Criminal [Triad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triad_\(organised_crime\)) groups, such as the [Wo Hop To](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wo_Hop_To), were active in San Francisco in the 20th century.[^cite_note-215]

According to statistics released by SFPD in April 2024, the crime figures were down in the first 100 days of the year, namely in terms of robberies, burglaries and larceny.[^cite_note-216] In the first half of 2024, San Francisco experienced a 32% decrease in overall crime compared to the previous year. Personal property theft saw a 41% reduction, and violent crimes, including gun-related incidents, decreased by 30%.[^cite_note-217]

## Economy

See also: [List of companies based in San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_companies_based_in_San_Francisco)



[San Francisco's Financial District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_District,_San_Francisco) is considered the [Wall Street of the West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wall_Street_West#%22Wall_Street_of_the_West%22).



Map of Jobs in the Bay Area by City and Sector

The city has a diversified [service economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Service_economy), with employment spread across a wide range of professional services, including [tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism), [financial services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_services), and [high technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_tech).[^cite_note-caedd-2016-218] In 2016, approximately 27% of workers were employed in professional business services; 14% in leisure and hospitality; 13% in government services; 12% in education and health care; 11% in trade, transportation, and utilities; and 8% in financial activities.[^cite_note-caedd-2016-218] In 2024, GDP in the nine-county [San Francisco Bay Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Bay_Area) grew 4.3% in real terms to \$1.332 trillion.[^cite_note-\:1-25]

In 2024, the 13-county [San Jose–San Francisco–Oakland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Jose%E2%80%93San_Francisco%E2%80%93Oakland,_CA_Combined_Statistical_Area) [combined statistical area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combined_statistical_area) had a GDP of \$1.408 trillion, ranking 3rd among [CSAs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combined_statistical_area), and ahead of all but [15 countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(nominal\)). As of 2024, San Francisco County ranked 9th by income per capita among U.S. counties, with a per capita personal income of \$171,497.[^cite_note-2021Pop-10] [Marin County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marin_County,_California), directly to the north over the [Golden Gate Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Gate_Bridge), and [San Mateo County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Mateo_County,_California), directly to the south on the [Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Peninsula), were the 7th and 8th highest-income counties respectively.



Skyline of [South of Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_of_Market,_San_Francisco) (SoMa), including [Salesforce Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salesforce_Tower), the [tallest building in San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_San_Francisco)

The legacy of the California gold rush turned San Francisco into the principal banking and finance center of the [West Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Coast_of_the_United_States) in the early twentieth century.[^cite_note-California2011-219] [Montgomery Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montgomery_Street) in the [Financial District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_District,_San_Francisco) became known as the "[Wall Street of the West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wall_Street_West#San_Francisco)", home to the [Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Reserve_Bank_of_San_Francisco), and the site of the now-defunct [Pacific Coast Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Exchange).[^cite_note-California2011-219] [Bank of America](347756.md), a pioneer in making banking services accessible to the middle class, was founded in San Francisco and in the 1960s, built the landmark modern skyscraper at [555 California Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/555_California_Street) for its corporate headquarters, since relocated to [Charlotte](57447.md).&#x20;

Many large financial institutions, multinational banks, and venture capital firms are based in or have regional headquarters in the city. With over 30 international financial institutions,[^cite_note-CityData.com-220] six [Fortune 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500) companies,[^cite_note-221] and a large supporting infrastructure of professional services—including law, public relations, [architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_architecture) and design—San Francisco is designated as an [Alpha(-) World City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalization_and_World_Cities_Research_Network#Alpha).[^cite_note-222] The 2024 [Global Financial Centres Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Financial_Centres_Index) ranked San Francisco as the fifth-most competitive financial center in the world.[^cite_note-\:2-223]

Beginning in the 1990s, San Francisco's economy diversified away from finance and tourism towards the growing fields of high tech, [biotechnology](4502.md), and [medical research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_research).[^cite_note-Waters2009-224] Technology jobs accounted for just 1 percent of San Francisco's economy in 1990, growing to 4 percent in 2010 and an estimated 8 percent by the end of 2013.[^cite_note-225] San Francisco became a center of Internet [start-up companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Startup_company) during the [dot-com bubble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dot-com_bubble) of the 1990s and the subsequent [social media](5897742.md) boom of the late 2000s (decade).[^cite_note-226] Since 2010, San Francisco proper has attracted an increasing share of venture capital investments as compared to nearby [Silicon Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_Valley), attracting 423 financings worth US\$4.58 billion in 2013.[^cite_note-227][^cite_note-228][^cite_note-229] In 2004, the city approved a payroll tax exemption for biotechnology companies[^cite_note-230] to foster growth in the [Mission Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mission_Bay,_San_Francisco) neighborhood, site of a second campus and hospital of the [University of California, San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California,_San_Francisco). Mission Bay hosts the [UCSF Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UCSF_Medical_Center), the [California Institute for Regenerative Medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Institute_for_Regenerative_Medicine), [California Institute for Quantitative Biosciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Institute_for_Quantitative_Biosciences), and [Gladstone Institutes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gladstone_Institutes),[^cite_note-231] as well as more than 40 private-sector life sciences companies.[^cite_note-232]



[Union Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Square,_San_Francisco) is a major [retail](183515.md) hub for San Francisco and the Bay Area.

According to academic Rob Wilson, San Francisco is a [global city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_city).[^cite_note-233] However, the [COVID-19 pandemic](63136490.md) has led to high office vacancy rates and the accelerating evacuation of many retail and tech businesses out of the downtown core of San Francisco.[^cite_note-DowntownSanFranDying-110][^cite_note-SanFran2025AcceleratingDoomLoop-234][^cite_note-SanFranExodus4-235][^cite_note-236] Attributed causes include a shift to [remote work](158555.md) in the technology and professional services sectors, as well as high levels of [homelessness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homelessness_in_the_San_Francisco_Bay_Area), drug use, and crime in areas around downtown San Francisco, such as the [Tenderloin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tenderloin,_San_Francisco) and [Mid-Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mid-Market,_San_Francisco) neighborhoods.[^cite_note-SanFranDoomLoop5-237][^cite_note-238]

The top employer in San Francisco is the city government itself, employing 5.6% (35,000+ people) of the city's workforce, followed by the [University of California, San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California,_San_Francisco) with over 29,000 employees.[^cite_note-239] The largest private-sector employer is [Salesforce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salesforce), with 11,953 employees, as of 2024. Small businesses with fewer than 10 employees and self-employed firms made up 85% of city establishments in 2006,[^cite_note-240] and the number of San Franciscans employed by firms of more than 1,000 employees has fallen by half since 1977.[^cite_note-ega06b-241] The growth of national [big box](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big-box_store) and [formula retail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_retail) chains into the city has been made intentionally difficult by political and civic consensus. In an effort to buoy small privately owned businesses in San Francisco and preserve the unique retail personality of the city, the Small Business Commission started a publicity campaign in 2004 to keep a larger share of retail dollars in the local economy,[^cite_note-242] and the Board of Supervisors has used the planning code to limit the neighborhoods where formula retail establishments can set up shop,[^cite_note-243] an effort affirmed by San Francisco voters.[^cite_note-244] However, by 2016, San Francisco was rated low by small businesses in a Business Friendliness Survey.[^cite_note-245]



[Ferry Building](1774164.md) in the [Embarcadero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embarcadero_\(San_Francisco\)).

[Like many U.S. cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manufacturing_in_the_United_States), San Francisco once had a significant manufacturing sector employing nearly 60,000 workers in 1969, but nearly all production left for cheaper locations by the 1980s.[^cite_note-Frojo2014-246] As of 2014, San Francisco has seen a small resurgence in manufacturing, with more than 4,000 manufacturing jobs across 500 companies, doubling since 2011. The city's largest manufacturing employer is [Anchor Brewing Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anchor_Brewing_Company), and the largest by revenue is [Timbuk2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timbuk2).[^cite_note-Frojo2014-246]

As of the first quarter of 2022, the median value of homes in San Francisco County was \$1,297,030. It ranked third in the U.S. for counties with highest median home value, behind [Nantucket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nantucket) and [San Mateo County, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Mateo_County,_California).[^cite_note-247]

### Technology



Former [X](9988187.md) headquarters on [Market Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_Street_\(San_Francisco\)), prior to moving to [Austin, Texas](1998.md)

San Francisco became a hub for technology-driven economic growth during the [internet boom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_boom) of the 1990s, and still holds an important position in the world city network today.[^cite_note-\:02-180][^cite_note-\:3-248] Intense redevelopment towards the "[new economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_economy)" makes business more technologically minded. Between the years of 1999 and 2000, the job growth rate was 4.9%, creating over 50,000 jobs in technology firms and internet content production.[^cite_note-\:02-180] However, the technology industry has become geographically dispersed.[^cite_note-NewYorkCloserThanEvertoBayAreaTech-249][^cite_note-ManhattanNowBiggestTechHub-250]

In the second technological boom driven by social media in the mid-2000s, San Francisco became a location for companies such as [Apple](856.md), [Google](1092923.md), [Ubisoft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ubisoft_San_Francisco), [Facebook](7529378.md), and [Twitter](9988187.md) (now known as *X*) to base their tech offices and for their employees to live.[^cite_note-ega06a-106]

The final wave of technology materialized as AI companies have either been founded in the city or moved there attracted to a large talent pool, proximity to venture capital, and serendipitous connections.[^cite_note-251]

The [Sunset Reservoir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunset_Reservoir) Solar Project, completed in 2010, installed 24,000 solar panels on the roof of the reservoir. The 5-megawatt plant more than tripled the city's 2-megawatt solar generation capacity when it opened in December 2010.[^cite_note-252][^cite_note-253]

### Tourism and conventions

See also: [Port of San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_San_Francisco)



The [Fisherman's Wharf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fisherman's_Wharf,_San_Francisco) is a popular tourist attraction.

Tourism is one of San Francisco's most important private-sector industries, accounting for more than one out of seven jobs in the city.[^cite_note-Waters2009-224][^cite_note-tourism-largest-private-sector-254] The city's [frequent portrayal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_in_popular_culture) in music, film, and popular culture has made the city and its landmarks recognizable worldwide. In 2016, it attracted the fifth-highest number of foreign tourists of any city in the United States.[^cite_note-255] In 2023, 23.1 million visitors arrived in San Francisco, adding US\$9.3 billion to the economy.[^cite_note-256] With a large hotel infrastructure and a major convention facility in the [Moscone Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moscone_Center), San Francisco is a popular destination for annual conventions and conferences.[^cite_note-257]

Some of the most popular tourist attractions in San Francisco, as noted by the [Travel Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Travel_Channel), include the [Golden Gate Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Gate_Bridge) and [Alamo Square Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alamo_Square,_San_Francisco), home to the famous "[Painted Ladies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Painted_Ladies)". Both of these locations were often used as landscape shots for the hit American television sitcom *[Full House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Full_House)*. There is also [Lombard Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lombard_Street_\(San_Francisco\)), known for its "crookedness" and extensive views. Tourists also visit [Pier 39](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pier_39), which offers dining, shopping, entertainment, and views of the bay, sunbathing [California sea lions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_sea_lions), the [Aquarium of the Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aquarium_of_the_Bay), and the famous [Alcatraz Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcatraz_Island).[^cite_note-258]



[Coit Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coit_Tower) on [Telegraph Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telegraph_Hill,_San_Francisco)

San Francisco also offers tourists varied nightlife in its neighborhoods.[^cite_note-259][^cite_note-260]

The new Terminal Project at Pier 27 opened September 25, 2014, as a replacement for the old Pier 35.[^cite_note-261]

A heightened interest in conventioneering in San Francisco, marked by the establishment of convention centers such as Yerba Buena, acted as a feeder into the local tourist economy and resulted in an increase in the hotel industry: "In 1959, the city had fewer than thirty-three hundred first-class hotel rooms; by 1970, the number was nine thousand; and by 1999, there were more than thirty thousand."[^cite_note-262] The [commodification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pink_capitalism) of the [Castro District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Castro_District,_San_Francisco) has contributed to San Francisco's tourist economy.[^cite_note-263]

## Arts and culture

Main article: [Culture of San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_San_Francisco)

See also: [San Francisco in popular culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_in_popular_culture)



The [Palace of Fine Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Fine_Arts), originally built for the 1915 [Panama–Pacific International Exposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panama%E2%80%93Pacific_International_Exposition)

Although the [Financial District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_District,_San_Francisco), [Union Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Square,_San_Francisco), and [Fisherman's Wharf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fisherman's_Wharf,_San_Francisco) are well known around the world, San Francisco is also characterized by its numerous culturally rich streetscapes featuring [mixed-use](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mixed-use_development) neighborhoods anchored around [central commercial corridors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_streets_in_San_Francisco) to which residents and visitors alike can walk.[^cite_note-264] Because of these characteristics, San Francisco is ranked the "most walkable" city in the United States by [Walk Score](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walk_Score).[^cite_note-265]

Many neighborhoods feature a mix of businesses, restaurants and venues that cater to the daily needs of local residents while also serving many visitors and tourists. Some neighborhoods are dotted with boutiques, cafés and nightlife such as Union Street in [Cow Hollow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cow_Hollow,_San_Francisco), 24th Street in [Noe Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noe_Valley,_San_Francisco), [Valencia Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valencia_Street) in the [Mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mission_District,_San_Francisco), Grant Avenue in [North Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Beach,_San_Francisco), and Irving Street in the [Inner Sunset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunset_District,_San_Francisco). This approach especially has influenced the continuing South of Market neighborhood redevelopment with businesses and neighborhood services rising alongside high-rise residences.[^cite_note-FogDev-266]



[The Castro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Castro_District,_San_Francisco) is famous as one of the first [gay villages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gay_village) in the country.

[^cite_note-SFGateCastro-130]

Since the 1990s, the demand for skilled [information technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Information_technology) workers from local startups and nearby [Silicon Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_Valley) has attracted [white-collar workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White-collar_worker) from all over the world and created a high standard of living in San Francisco.[^cite_note-267] Many neighborhoods that were once [blue-collar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue-collar), middle, and lower class have been [gentrifying](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gentrification), as many of the city's traditional business and industrial districts have experienced a renaissance driven by the redevelopment of the [Embarcadero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embarcadero_\(San_Francisco\)), including the neighborhoods [South Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Beach,_San_Francisco) and [Mission Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mission_Bay,_San_Francisco). The city's property values and household income have risen to among the highest in the nation,[^cite_note-268][^cite_note-MedianIncome-269][^cite_note-270] creating a large and upscale restaurant, retail, and entertainment scene. According to a 2014 quality of life survey of global cities, San Francisco has the [highest quality of living](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Most_livable_cities) of any U.S. city.[^cite_note-271] However, due to the exceptionally high cost of living, many of the city's middle and lower-class families have been leaving the city for the outer suburbs of the [Bay Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Bay_Area), or for California's [Central Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Valley_\(California\)).[^cite_note-MiddleClass-272] By June 2, 2015, the median rent was reported to be as high as \$4,225.[^cite_note-273] The high cost of living is due in part to restrictive planning laws which limit new residential construction.[^cite_note-274]



The [Mission District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mission_District,_San_Francisco) is the historic center of the city's [Chicano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicano)/[Mexican-American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican-American) population and greater [Hispanic and Latino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanics_and_Latinos_in_California) community.

The international character that San Francisco has enjoyed since its founding is continued today by large numbers of immigrants from Asia and Latin America. With 39% of its residents born overseas,[^cite_note-ega06b-241] San Francisco has numerous neighborhoods filled with businesses and civic institutions catering to new arrivals. In particular, the arrival of many ethnic Chinese, which began to accelerate in the 1970s, has complemented the long-established community historically based in [Chinatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown,_San_Francisco) throughout the city and has transformed the annual [Chinese New Year Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Chinese_New_Year_Festival_and_Parade) into the largest lunar new year event outside of Asia.[^cite_note-275]

With the arrival of the ["beat"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beat_Generation) writers and artists of the 1950s and societal changes culminating in the [Summer of Love](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summer_of_Love) in the [Haight-Ashbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haight-Ashbury) district during the 1960s, San Francisco became a center of [liberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_liberalism_in_the_United_States) activism and of the [counterculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counterculture_of_the_1960s) that arose at that time. The [Democrats](5043544.md) and to a lesser extent the [Green Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Party_\(United_States\)) have dominated [city politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_San_Francisco) since the late 1970s, after the [last serious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Barbagelata) [Republican](32070.md) challenger for city office [lost the 1975 mayoral election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1975_San_Francisco_mayoral_election) by a narrow margin. San Francisco has not voted more than 20% for a [Republican](32070.md) presidential or senatorial candidate since [1988](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_United_States_presidential_election).[^cite_note-276] In 2007, the city expanded its [Medicaid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medicaid) and other [indigent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigent) medical programs into the [Healthy San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthy_San_Francisco) program,[^cite_note-277] which [subsidizes](146719.md) certain medical services for eligible residents.[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279][^cite_note-280]



The [San Francisco Museum of Modern Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Museum_of_Modern_Art), also known as SF MOMA

Since 1993, the San Francisco Department of Public Health has distributed 400,000 [free syringes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Needle_and_syringe_programmes#San_Francisco) every month aimed at reducing HIV and other health risks for drug users, as well as providing disposal sites and services.[^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283]

San Francisco also has had a very active environmental community. Starting with the founding of the [Sierra Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sierra_Club) in 1892 to the establishment of the non-profit [Friends of the Urban Forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friends_of_the_Urban_Forest) in 1981, San Francisco has been at the forefront of many global discussions regarding the environment.[^cite_note-284][^cite_note-285] The 1980 [San Francisco Recycling Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Mandatory_Recycling_and_Composting_Ordinance) was one of the earliest curbside recycling programs.[^cite_note-286] The city's GoSolarSF incentive promotes solar installations and the [San Francisco Public Utilities Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Public_Utilities_Commission) is rolling out the [CleanPowerSF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CleanPowerSF) program to sell electricity from local renewable sources.[^cite_note-287][^cite_note-288] SF Greasecycle is a program to recycle used cooking oil for conversion to biodiesel.[^cite_note-289]

### LGBTQ

Main article: [LGBTQ culture in San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_culture_in_San_Francisco)



[San Francisco Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Pride) is one of the oldest and largest [LGBTQ pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pride_\(LGBTQ_culture\)) events in the world.

San Francisco has long had an [LGBTQ](78174325.md)-friendly [history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_culture_in_San_Francisco). It was home to the first lesbian-rights organization in the United States, [Daughters of Bilitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daughters_of_Bilitis); the first openly gay person to run for public office in the United States, [José Sarria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Sarria); the first openly gay man to be elected to public office in California, [Harvey Milk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvey_Milk); the first openly lesbian judge appointed in the U.S., [Mary C. Morgan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_C._Morgan); and the first [transgender](19904525.md) police commissioner, [Theresa Sparks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theresa_Sparks).[^cite_note-thetravelintern-290]

San Francisco was home to the first lesbian bar in America, Mona's, on Union Street. The bar aimed to stand out as a space where women could be served by women wearing tuxedos, playing to the exclusion of men in a sapphic space, and catered to local queer patrons instead of the tourist ideal. As the bar continued to see more clientele, the booming business relocated to its more permanent location at 440 Broadway. Here, the business underwent a name change – now [Mona's 440 Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mona's_440_Club) – but continued hosting drag artists and various headliners. Most namely, [Gladys Bentley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gladys_Bentley), a drag king pioneer and performance artist,[^cite_note-291] quickly identified for her unofficial tuxedo and top hat attire. Despite being the first [lesbian bar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lesbian_bar) in the nation, it was eventually sold and rebranded under new management. Since its closing in the 1950s, other iconic sapphic bars in San Francisco have emerged, including [Wild Side West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wild_Side_West),[^cite_note-292] [Maud's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maud's_\(bar\)), and [The Lexington Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Lexington_Club). More recently, Rikki's, the first and only women's sports bar, opened in Summer 2025.[^cite_note-293] Drag artists and live entertainment continue to be a thriving part of sapphic and queer spaces in the city and, more notably, [The Castro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Castro_District,_San_Francisco). Today, sapphic spaces, even in San Francisco, continue struggling to keep their doors open and remain in decline in comparison to gay spaces.[^cite_note-294]

The city's large gay population has created and sustained a politically and culturally active community over many decades, developing a powerful presence in San Francisco's civic life.[^cite_note-thetravelintern-290] Survey data released in 2015 by [Gallup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup_\(company\)) places the proportion of LGBTQ adults in the San Francisco metro area at 6.2%, which is the highest proportion of the 50 most populous metropolitan areas as measured by the polling organization.[^cite_note-295]



The [gay pride flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rainbow_flag_\(LGBTQ\)) was originally developed in San Francisco.

One of the most popular destinations for gay tourists internationally, the city hosts [San Francisco Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Pride), one of the largest and oldest [pride parades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pride_parades). San Francisco Pride events have been held continuously since 1972. The events are themed and a new theme is created each year.[^cite_note-296] In 2013, over 1.5 million people attended, around 500,000 more than the previous year.[^cite_note-297] [Pink Saturday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pink_Saturday) is an annual street party held the Saturday before the pride parade, which coincides with the [Dyke march](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dyke_march).

The [Folsom Street Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folsom_Street_Fair) (FSF) is an annual [BDSM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BDSM) and [leather subculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leather_subculture) street fair that is held in September, endcapping San Francisco's "Leather Pride Week".[^cite_note-298] It started in 1984 and is California's third-largest single-day, outdoor spectator event and the world's largest leather event and showcase for BDSM products and culture.[^cite_note-299]

### Performing arts

See also: [List of theatres in San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_theatres_in_San_Francisco)



[War Memorial Opera House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_Memorial_Opera_House), part of the [S.F. War Memorial & Performing Arts Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_War_Memorial_and_Performing_Arts_Center), one of the largest performing arts centers in the U.S.



[Golden Gate Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Gate_Theatre) is located in the historic [Theatre District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theatre_District,_San_Francisco)

San Francisco's [War Memorial and Performing Arts Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_War_Memorial_and_Performing_Arts_Center) hosts some of the most enduring performing arts companies in the country. The [War Memorial Opera House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_Memorial_Opera_House) houses the [San Francisco Opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Opera), the second-largest opera company in North America[^cite_note-300] as well as the [San Francisco Ballet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Ballet), while the [San Francisco Symphony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Symphony) plays in [Davies Symphony Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louise_M._Davies_Symphony_Hall). Opened in 2013, the [SFJAZZ Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SFJAZZ_Center) hosts jazz performances year round.[^cite_note-301]

[The Fillmore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Fillmore) is a music venue located in the [Western Addition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Addition,_San_Francisco). It is the second incarnation of the historic venue that gained fame in the 1960s, housing the stage where now-famous musicians such as the [Grateful Dead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grateful_Dead), [Janis Joplin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janis_Joplin), [Led Zeppelin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Led_Zeppelin), and [Jefferson Airplane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jefferson_Airplane) first performed, fostering the [San Francisco Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Sound).[^cite_note-302] It closed its doors in 1971 with a final performance by [Santana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santana_\(band\)) and reopened in 1994 with a show by [the Smashing Pumpkins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Smashing_Pumpkins).[^cite_note-303]

San Francisco has a large number of [theaters and live performance venues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_theatres_in_San_Francisco). Local theater companies have been noted for risk taking and innovation.[^cite_note-304] The [Tony Award](54741.md)-winning non-profit [American Conservatory Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Conservatory_Theater) (A.C.T.) is a member of the national [League of Resident Theatres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_of_Resident_Theatres). Other local winners of the [Regional Theatre Tony Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_Theatre_Tony_Award) include the [San Francisco Mime Troupe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Mime_Troupe).[^cite_note-tbtb-305] San Francisco theaters frequently host pre-[Broadway](725252.md) engagements and tryout runs,[^cite_note-306] and some original San Francisco productions have later moved to Broadway.[^cite_note-tbtb-305]

### Museums

Further information: [List of museums in San Francisco Bay Area, California § San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_museums_in_San_Francisco_Bay_Area,_California#San_Francisco)



The [California Palace of the Legion of Honor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legion_of_Honor_\(museum\)), part of the [Fine Arts Museums of San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fine_Arts_Museums_of_San_Francisco)

The [San Francisco Museum of Modern Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Museum_of_Modern_Art) (SFMOMA) houses 20th century and contemporary works of art. It moved to its current building in the [South of Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_of_Market,_San_Francisco) neighborhood in 1995 and attracted more than 600,000 visitors annually.[^cite_note-307] SFMOMA closed for renovation and expansion in 2013. The museum reopened in May 2016, with an addition, designed by [Snøhetta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sn%C3%B8hetta_\(company\)), that has doubled the museum's size.[^cite_note-308]

The [Palace of the Legion of Honor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Palace_of_the_Legion_of_Honor) holds primarily European antiquities and works of art at its [Lincoln Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Park_\(San_Francisco\)) building modeled after its [Parisian namesake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palais_de_la_L%C3%A9gion_d'Honneur). The [de Young Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M._H._de_Young_Memorial_Museum) in Golden Gate Park features American decorative pieces and anthropological holdings from Africa, Oceania and the Americas, while Asian art is housed in the [Asian Art Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Art_Museum_of_San_Francisco). Opposite the de Young stands the [California Academy of Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Academy_of_Sciences), a natural history museum that also hosts the [Morrison Planetarium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morrison_Planetarium) and [Steinhart Aquarium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steinhart_Aquarium).[^cite_note-309]

Located on Pier 15 on the Embarcadero, the [Exploratorium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exploratorium) is an interactive science museum. The [Contemporary Jewish Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_Jewish_Museum) is a non-collecting institution that hosts a broad array of temporary exhibitions. On Nob Hill, the [Cable Car Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Cable_Car_Museum) is a working museum featuring the cable car powerhouse, which drives the cables.[^cite_note-310] [Wattis Institute for Contemporary Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wattis_Institute_for_Contemporary_Arts) was founded in 1998 and is part of the California College of the Arts.[^cite_note-311]

## Sports

Further information: [Sports in the San Francisco Bay Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_the_San_Francisco_Bay_Area)



[Oracle Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oracle_Park), home of the [SF Giants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Giants)

[Major League Baseball](38776.md)'s [San Francisco Giants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Giants) have played in San Francisco since moving from New York in 1958. The Giants play at [Oracle Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oracle_Park), which opened in 2000.[^cite_note-312] The Giants won [World Series](23489161.md) titles in [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_World_Series), [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_World_Series), and in [2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_World_Series). The Giants have boasted stars such as [Willie Mays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willie_Mays), [Willie McCovey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willie_McCovey), and [Barry Bonds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Bonds) (MLB's [career home run leader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Major_League_Baseball_career_home_run_leaders)). In 2012, San Francisco was ranked No. 1 in a study that examined which U.S. metro areas have produced the most Major Leaguers since 1920.[^cite_note-313]

The [San Francisco 49ers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_49ers) of the [National Football League](21211.md) (NFL) began playing in 1946 as an [All-America Football Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-America_Football_Conference) (AAFC) league charter member, moved to the NFL in 1950 and into [Candlestick Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candlestick_Park) in 1971. The team left San Francisco in 2014, moving approximately 50 miles south to [Santa Clara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Clara,_California), and began playing its home games at [Levi's Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levi's_Stadium),[^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315] Despite this, the 49ers are still branded as a San Francisco team, and when the team hosted [Super Bowl 50](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_50) events were hosted in San Francisco such as the annual [NFL Experience](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Football_League_Experience) which was held at the [Moscone Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moscone_Center) and "Super Bowl City" which opened on January 30 at Justin Herman Plaza on [The Embarcadero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embarcadero_\(San_Francisco\)).[^cite_note-316][^cite_note-317] The 49ers have won five [Super Bowl](27718.md) titles between 1982 and 1995.



[Chase Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chase_Center), home of the [Golden State Warriors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_State_Warriors)

The NBA's Golden State Warriors have played in the San Francisco Bay Area since moving from Philadelphia in 1962. The Warriors played as the San Francisco Warriors, from 1962 to 1971, before being renamed the [Golden State Warriors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_State_Warriors) prior to the 1971–1972 season in an attempt to present the team as a representation of the whole state of California, which had already adopted "The Golden State" nickname.[^cite_note-318] The Warriors' arena, [Chase Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chase_Center), is located in San Francisco.[^cite_note-319] After winning two championships in Philadelphia, they have won five championships since moving to the San Francisco Bay Area,[^cite_note-320] and made five consecutive [NBA Finals](890793.md) from 2015 to 2019, winning three of them. They won again in 2022, the franchise's first championship while residing in San Francisco proper.

At the collegiate level, the [San Francisco Dons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Dons) compete in [NCAA Division I](669931.md). [Bill Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Russell) led the Dons basketball team to [NCAA championships](507399.md) in 1955 and 1956. There is also the [San Francisco State Gators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_State_Gators), who compete in [NCAA Division II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCAA_Division_II).[^cite_note-321] Oracle Park hosted the annual [Fight Hunger Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fight_Hunger_Bowl) college football game from 2002 through 2013 before it moved to Santa Clara.

There are a handful of lower-league soccer clubs in San Francisco playing mostly from April – June.

| Club | Founded | Venue | League | Tier level |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [El Farolito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Farolito_SC) | 1985 | [Boxer Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boxer_Stadium) | [NPSL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Professional_Soccer_League_\(1967\)) | 4 |
| [San Francisco City FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_City_FC) | 2001 | [Kezar Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kezar_Stadium) | [USL League Two](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USL_League_Two) | 4 |
| [San Francisco Glens SC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Glens_SC) | 1961 | [Skyline College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skyline_College) | [USL League Two](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USL_League_Two) | 4 |



[Bay to Breakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_to_Breakers) is an annual foot race known for colorful costumes.

The [Bay to Breakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_to_Breakers) footrace, held annually since 1912, is best known for colorful costumes and a celebratory community spirit.[^cite_note-322] The [San Francisco Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Marathon) attracts more than 21,000 participants.[^cite_note-323] The [Escape from Alcatraz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Escape_from_Alcatraz_\(triathlon\)) [triathlon](31524.md) has, since 1980, attracted 2,000 top professional and amateur triathletes for its annual race.[^cite_note-324] The [Olympic Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_Club), founded in 1860, is the oldest [athletic club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_club) in the United States. Its private golf course has hosted the [U.S. Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Open_\(golf\)) on five occasions. San Francisco hosted the [2013 America's Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_America's_Cup) yacht racing competition.[^cite_note-325]

With an ideal climate for outdoor activities, San Francisco has ample resources and opportunities for amateur and participatory sports and recreation. There are more than 200 miles (320 km) of [bicycle paths, lanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Segregated_cycle_facilities) and bike routes in the city.[^cite_note-326] San Francisco residents have often ranked among the fittest in the country.[^cite_note-fitness-327] [Golden Gate Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Gate_Park) has miles of paved and unpaved running trails as well as a [golf course](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golf_course) and [disc golf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disc_golf) course. Boating, sailing, [windsurfing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windsurfing) and [kitesurfing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kitesurfing) are among the popular activities on San Francisco Bay, and the city maintains a yacht harbor in the [Marina District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marina_District,_San_Francisco).

San Francisco also has had [Esports](564204.md) teams, such as the [Overwatch League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overwatch_League)'s [San Francisco Shock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Shock). Established in 2017,[^cite_note-328] they won two back-to-back championship titles in 2019 and 2020.[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330]

## Parks and recreation

See also: [List of parks in San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_parks_in_San_Francisco)



[Golden Gate Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Gate_Park) is the 3rd most-visited [city park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_park) in the U.S., after [Central Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Park) and the [National Mall](167796.md).

[^cite_note-331]

Several of San Francisco's parks and nearly all of its beaches form part of the regional [Golden Gate National Recreation Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Gate_National_Recreation_Area), one of the most visited units of the [National Park system](161535.md) in the United States with over 13 million visitors a year. Among the GGNRA's attractions within the city are [Ocean Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ocean_Beach,_San_Francisco), which runs along the Pacific Ocean shoreline and is frequented by a [surfing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surfing) community, and [Baker Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baker_Beach), which is located in a cove west of the Golden Gate Bridge, as well as the [California Academy of Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Academy_of_Sciences), a research institute and natural history museum.

The [Presidio of San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidio_of_San_Francisco) is the former 18th century Spanish military base, which today is one of the city's largest parks and home to numerous museums and institutions. Also within the Presidio is [Crissy Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crissy_Field), a former airfield that was restored to its natural [salt marsh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salt_marsh) [ecosystem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecosystem). The GGNRA also administers [Fort Funston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Funston), [Lands End](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lands_End_\(San_Francisco\)), [Fort Mason](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Mason), and [Alcatraz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcatraz). The National Park Service separately administers the [San Francisco Maritime National Historical Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Maritime_National_Historical_Park) – a fleet of historic ships and waterfront property around [Aquatic Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aquatic_Park_Historic_District).[^cite_note-332]



[Painted Ladies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Painted_Ladies) on [Alamo Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alamo_Square).



The [Cliff House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cliff_House,_San_Francisco) over [Ocean Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ocean_Beach,_San_Francisco)

There are more than [220 parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_parks_in_San_Francisco) maintained by the [San Francisco Recreation & Parks Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Recreation_&_Parks_Department).[^cite_note-333] The largest and best-known city park is [Golden Gate Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Gate_Park),[^cite_note-334] which stretches from the center of the city west to the Pacific Ocean. Once covered in native grasses and sand dunes, the park was conceived in the 1860s and was created by the extensive planting of thousands of non-native trees and plants. The large park is rich with cultural and natural attractions such as the [Conservatory of Flowers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatory_of_Flowers), [Japanese Tea Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Tea_Garden_\(San_Francisco\)) and [San Francisco Botanical Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Botanical_Garden).[^cite_note-335] The [Dahlia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dahlia) is the official flower of San Francisco, while the [red-masked parakeet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red-masked_parakeet) is its official animal.

[Lake Merced](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Merced) is a fresh-water lake surrounded by parkland[^cite_note-336] and near the [San Francisco Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Zoo), a city-owned park that houses more than 250 animal species, many of which are endangered.[^cite_note-337] The only [park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_California_state_parks) managed by the [California State Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Department_of_Parks_and_Recreation) system located principally in San Francisco, [Candlestick Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candlestick_Point_State_Recreation_Area) was the state's first urban recreation area.[^cite_note-338]

Most of San Francisco's islands are protected as parkland or nature reserves. [Alcatraz Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcatraz_Island), operated by the [National Park Service](161535.md), is open to the public. The [Farallon Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farallon_Islands) are protected wildlife refuges. The [Seal Rocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seal_Rocks_\(San_Francisco,_California\)) are protected as part of [Golden Gate National Recreation Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Gate_National_Recreation_Area). [Red Rock Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Rock_Island) is the only privately owned island in San Francisco Bay, but is uninhabited. [Yerba Buena Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yerba_Buena_Island) is largely utilized by the military.

San Francisco is the first city in the U.S. to have a park within a [10-Minute Walk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/10-Minute_Walk) of every resident.[^cite_note-339][^cite_note-340] It also ranks fifth in the U.S. for park access and quality in the 2018 ParkScore ranking of the top 100 park systems across the United States, according to the nonprofit [Trust for Public Land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trust_for_Public_Land).[^cite_note-341]

## Government

Main articles: [Government of San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_San_Francisco), [Politics of San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_San_Francisco), and [Mayors of San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_San_Francisco)

See also: [San Francisco City Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_City_Hall)



[San Francisco City Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_City_Hall), built 1913–16 and designed by [Arthur Brown Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Brown_Jr.)

The [mayor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_San_Francisco) is also the county executive, and the county [Board of Supervisors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Board_of_Supervisors) acts as the [city council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_council). The government of San Francisco is a [charter city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charter_city) and is constituted of two co-equal branches: the executive branch is headed by the mayor and includes other citywide elected and appointed officials as well as the civil service; the 11-member Board of Supervisors, the legislative branch, is headed by a president and is responsible for passing laws and budgets, though San Franciscans also make use of [direct ballot initiatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_initiative) to pass legislation.[^cite_note-342]

Because of its unique city-county status, the local government is able to exercise jurisdiction over certain property outside city limits. [San Francisco International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_International_Airport), though located in [San Mateo County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Mateo_County), is owned and operated by the City and County of San Francisco. San Francisco's largest jail complex (County Jail No. 5) is located in San Mateo County, in an [unincorporated area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unincorporated_area) adjacent to [San Bruno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Bruno,_California). San Francisco was also granted a perpetual leasehold over the [Hetch Hetchy Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hetch_Hetchy_Valley) and [watershed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drainage_basin) in [Yosemite National Park](48664.md) by the [Raker Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raker_Act) in 1913.[^cite_note-CityCounty-343]



The [Supreme Court of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_California) is based in the [Earl Warren Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earl_Warren_Building).

The members of the Board of Supervisors are elected as representatives of specific districts within the city.[^cite_note-SF_Gov_Supervisor_Districts-344] Upon the death or resignation of the mayor, the President of the Board of Supervisors becomes acting mayor until the full Board elects an interim replacement for the remainder of the term. In 2011, [Ed Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ed_Lee_\(politician\)) was selected by the board to finish the term of [Gavin Newsom](418947.md), who resigned to take office as [Lieutenant Governor of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lieutenant_Governor_of_California).[^cite_note-345] Lee (who won two elections to remain mayor) was temporarily replaced by San Francisco Board of Supervisors President [London Breed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_Breed) after he died on December 12, 2017. Supervisor [Mark Farrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Farrell_\(politician\)) was appointed by the Board of Supervisors to finish Lee's term on January 23, 2018.

Most local offices in San Francisco are elected using [ranked choice voting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranked-choice_voting_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-346]



[San Francisco Federal Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Federal_Building)

San Francisco serves as the regional hub for many arms of the federal bureaucracy, including the [U.S. Court of Appeals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ninth_Circuit_U.S._Court_of_Appeals), the [Federal Reserve Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Reserve_Bank_of_San_Francisco), and the [U.S. Mint](358818.md). Until [decommissioning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Base_Realignment_and_Closure) in the early 1990s, the city had major military installations at the [Presidio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidio_of_San_Francisco), [Treasure Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treasure_Island,_San_Francisco), and [Hunters Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Naval_Shipyard)—a legacy still reflected in the annual celebration of [Fleet Week](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fleet_Week). The State of California uses San Francisco as the home of the [state supreme court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_California) and other state agencies. Foreign governments maintain [more than seventy consulates in San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_in_the_San_Francisco_Bay_Area).[^cite_note-347]

The municipal budget for fiscal year 2015–16 was \$8.99 billion,[^cite_note-348] and is one of the largest city budgets in the United States.[^cite_note-ballotpedia.org-349] The City of San Francisco spends more per resident than any city other than Washington, D.C., over \$10,000 in FY 2015–2016.[^cite_note-ballotpedia.org-349] The city employs around 27,000 workers.[^cite_note-350]



The historic [Browning Courthouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_R._Browning_United_States_Court_of_Appeals_Building)

In the [California State Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_Senate), San Francisco is in [the 11th senatorial district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California's_11th_senatorial_district), represented by [Democrat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Democratic_Party) [Scott Wiener](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scott_Wiener). In the [California State Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_Assembly), it is split between [the 17th Assembly district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California's_17th_State_Assembly_district), represented by [Democrat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Democratic_Party) [Matt Haney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matt_Haney), and [the 19th Assembly district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California's_19th_State_Assembly_district), represented by [Democrat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Democratic_Party) [Catherine Stefani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catherine_Stefani).[^cite_note-CCRC_CoI-351]

In the [United States House of Representatives](19468510.md), San Francisco is split between two congressional districts. Most of the city is in the [11th District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California's_11th_congressional_district), represented by [Nancy Pelosi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Pelosi) ([D](5043544.md)–[San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco,_California)). A sliver in the southwest is part of the [15th District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California's_15th_congressional_district) represented by [Kevin Mullin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Mullin) ([D](5043544.md)–[South San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_San_Francisco,_California)).[^cite_note-352]

## Education

### Colleges and universities

See also: [List of colleges and universities in San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_colleges_and_universities_in_San_Francisco)



The [University of San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_San_Francisco)

The [University of California, San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California,_San_Francisco), is the sole campus of the [University of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California) system entirely dedicated to graduate education in health and biomedical sciences. It is ranked among the top five medical schools in the United States[^cite_note-353] and operates the [UCSF Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UCSF_Medical_Center), which ranks as the number one hospital in California and the number 5 in the country.[^cite_note-354] UCSF is a major local employer, second in size only to the city and county government.[^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356][^cite_note-357] A 43-acre (17 ha) [Mission Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mission_Bay,_San_Francisco) campus was opened in 2003, complementing its original facility in [Parnassus Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_hills_in_San_Francisco). It contains research space and facilities to foster biotechnology and life sciences entrepreneurship and has doubled the size of UCSF's research enterprise.[^cite_note-358] All in all, UCSF operates more than 20 facilities across San Francisco.[^cite_note-359]

The [University of California College of the Law, San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California_College_of_the_Law,_San_Francisco), founded in [Civic Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civic_Center,_San_Francisco) in 1878, is the oldest law school in California and claims more judges on the state bench than any other institution.[^cite_note-360] San Francisco's two University of California institutions have recently formed an official affiliation in the UCSF/UC Law SF Consortium on Law, Science & Health Policy.[^cite_note-361]



The [San Francisco State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_State_University) library building

[San Francisco State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_State_University) is part of the [California State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_University) system and is located near [Lake Merced](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Merced).[^cite_note-sfsu-362] The school has approximately 30,000 students and awards undergraduate, master's and doctoral degrees in more than 100 disciplines.[^cite_note-sfsu-362] The [City College of San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_College_of_San_Francisco), with its main facility in the Ingleside district, is one of the largest two-year [community colleges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_college) in the country. It has an enrollment of about 100,000 students and offers an extensive continuing education program.[^cite_note-363]



The [University of California College of the Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California_College_of_the_Law,_San_Francisco)

Founded in 1855, the [University of San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_San_Francisco), a private [Jesuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Society_of_Jesus) university located on [Lone Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lone_Mountain_\(California\)), is the oldest institution of higher education in San Francisco and one of the oldest universities established west of the Mississippi River.[^cite_note-364] [Golden Gate University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Gate_University) is a private, nonsectarian university formed in 1901 and located in the [Financial District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_District,_San_Francisco).

[California Institute of Integral Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Institute_of_Integral_Studies), founded in 1968, offers graduate programs in its Schools of Professional Psychology & Health, and Consciousness and Transformation.

With an enrollment of 13,000 students, the [Academy of Art University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_of_Art_University) is the largest institute of art and design in the nation.[^cite_note-365] The [California College of the Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_College_of_the_Arts), located north of [Potrero Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potrero_Hill), has programs in architecture, fine arts, design, and writing.[^cite_note-366] The [San Francisco Conservatory of Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Conservatory_of_Music), the only independent [music school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_school) on the West Coast, grants degrees in orchestral instruments, chamber music, composition, and conducting. The [San Francisco Art Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Art_Institute), founded in 1871 and the oldest [art school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_school) west of the Mississippi,[^cite_note-367] closed in 2022.

The [California Culinary Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Culinary_Academy) had programs in the culinary arts, baking and pastry arts, and hospitality and restaurant management, and is now closed.

### Primary and secondary schools

See also: [San Francisco public grammar schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_public_grammar_schools) and [List of high schools in California § San Francisco County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_high_schools_in_California#San_Francisco_County)



The [San Francisco Unified School District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Unified_School_District) operates 114 schools and is the oldest school district in California.

[Public schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_school_\(government_funded\)) are run by the [San Francisco Unified School District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Unified_School_District), which covers the entire city and county,[^cite_note-368] as well as the [California State Board of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_Board_of_Education) for some charter schools. [Lowell High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lowell_High_School_\(San_Francisco\)), the oldest public high school in the U.S. west of the [Mississippi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_River),[^cite_note-369] and the smaller [School of the Arts High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruth_Asawa_San_Francisco_School_of_the_Arts) are two of San Francisco's [magnet schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magnet_school) at the secondary level. Public school students attend schools based on an assignment system rather than neighborhood proximity.[^cite_note-370]

Just under 30% of the city's school-age population attends one of San Francisco's more than 100 [private](612857.md) or [parochial schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parochial_school), compared to a 10% rate nationwide.[^cite_note-SFGPrvtSchools-371] Nearly 40 of those schools are [Catholic schools](1452366.md) managed by the [Archdiocese of San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_San_Francisco).[^cite_note-372]

San Francisco has nearly 300 [preschool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preschool) programs primarily operated by [Head Start](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_Start_\(program\)), [San Francisco Unified School District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Unified_School_District), private for-profit, private non-profit and [family child care](389615.md#Family_child_care_homes) providers.[^cite_note-373] All four-year-old children living in San Francisco are offered [universal access to preschool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_preschool).[^cite_note-374]

## Media

Further information: [Media in the San Francisco Bay Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_the_San_Francisco_Bay_Area)

Unsourced material may be challenged and [removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence). *(January 2022)*_&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Maintenance_template_removal))_ |
