The [San Francisco Chronicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Chronicle) building

The major daily newspaper in San Francisco is the *[San Francisco Chronicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Chronicle)*, which is currently Northern California's most widely circulated newspaper.[^cite_note-375] The Chronicle is most famous for a former columnist, the late [Herb Caen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herb_Caen), whose daily musings attracted critical acclaim and represented the "voice of San Francisco". *[The San Francisco Examiner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_San_Francisco_Examiner)*, once the cornerstone of [William Randolph Hearst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Randolph_Hearst)'s media empire and the home of [Ambrose Bierce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ambrose_Bierce), declined in circulation over the years and now takes the form of a free daily tabloid, under new ownership.[^cite_note-376][^cite_note-377]

*[Sing Tao Daily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sing_Tao_Daily)* claims to be the largest of several Chinese language dailies that serve the Bay Area.[^cite_note-378] *[SF Weekly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SF_Weekly)* is the city's [alternative weekly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alternative_weekly) newspaper. *[San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_\(magazine\))* and *[7x7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/7x7_\(magazine\))* are major glossy magazines about San Francisco. The national newsmagazine *[Mother Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother_Jones_\(magazine\))* is also based in San Francisco. San Francisco is home to online-only media publications such as [SFist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SFist), and *[AsianWeek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AsianWeek)*.



The [Julia Morgan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julia_Morgan)-designed Hearst Building, the western headquarters of the [Hearst Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hearst_Corporation)

The San Francisco Bay Area is the sixth-largest [television market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Designated_market_area) in the U.S.[^cite_note-379] It is the fourth-largest [radio market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Designated_market_area) after that of New York City, Los Angeles, and Chicago.[^cite_note-380]

All major U.S. television networks have [affiliates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_television_stations_in_the_San_Francisco_Bay_Area) serving the region, with most of them based in the city. [CNN](62028.md), [MSNBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MSNBC), [BBC](19344654.md), [Russia Today](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RT_\(TV_network\)), and [CCTV America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CCTV_America) also have regional news bureaus in San Francisco. [Bloomberg West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloomberg_West) was launched in 2011 from a studio on the Embarcadero and [CNBC](223441.md) broadcasts from [One Market Plaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Market_Plaza) since 2015. [ESPN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN) uses the local ABC studio for their broadcasting. The [regional sports network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_sports_network), [Comcast SportsNet Bay Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comcast_SportsNet_Bay_Area) and its sister station [Comcast SportsNet California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comcast_SportsNet_California), are both located in San Francisco. The [Pac-12 Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pac-12_Network) is also based in San Francisco.



[Sutro Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sutro_Tower) is a [broadcast tower](2044496.md) and local landmark.

[Public broadcasting](178808.md) outlets include both a [television station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KQED-TV) and a [radio station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KQED-FM), both broadcasting under the call letters KQED from a facility near the [Potrero Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potrero_Hill) neighborhood. KQED-FM is the most-listened-to [National Public Radio](29697232.md) affiliate in the country.[^cite_note-381]

[KUSF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KUSF_\(University_of_San_Francisco\)) is a student-run radio station by college students from the [University of San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_San_Francisco).[^cite_note-382] Another local broadcaster, [KPOO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KPOO), is an independent, African-American owned and operated noncommercial radio station established in 1971.[^cite_note-383] [CNET](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CNET_Networks), founded 1994, and [Salon.com](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salon.com), 1995, are based in San Francisco. [Sutro Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sutro_Tower) is an important [broadcast tower](2044496.md) located between [Mount Sutro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Sutro) and the [Twin Peaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twin_Peaks_\(San_Francisco\)), built in 1973 for [KTVU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KTVU), [KRON](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KRON), and [KPIX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KPIX).

## Infrastructure

### Transportation

See also: [Transportation in the San Francisco Bay Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transportation_in_the_San_Francisco_Bay_Area)

#### Public transportation

See also: [San Francisco Municipal Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Municipal_Railway)



A [San Francisco cable car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_cable_car_system) with [Alcatraz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcatraz) seen behind

Transit is the most used form of transportation every day in San Francisco. Every weekday, more than 560,000 people travel on Muni's 69 bus routes and more than 140,000 customers ride the Muni Metro light rail system.[^cite_note-\:0-384] 32% of San Francisco residents use public transportation for their daily commute to work, ranking it fourth in the United States and first on the West Coast.[^cite_note-385] The [San Francisco Municipal Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Municipal_Railway), primarily known as Muni, is the primary public transit system of San Francisco. As of 2023, Muni is the eighth-largest transit system in the United States.[^cite_note-386]

The system operates a combined [light rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Light_rail) and [heavy rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapid_transit) (subway) system, the [Muni Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muni_Metro), as well as large bus and [trolley coach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trolleybuses_in_San_Francisco) networks.[^cite_note-transportation-387] Additionally, it runs a [historic streetcar line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F_Market), which runs on Market Street from [Castro Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Castro) to [Fisherman's Wharf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fisherman's_Wharf,_San_Francisco).[^cite_note-transportation-387] It also operates the famous [cable cars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_cable_car_system),[^cite_note-transportation-387] which have been designated as a [National Historic Landmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Historic_Landmark) and are a major tourist attraction.[^cite_note-388]

[Bay Area Rapid Transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_Area_Rapid_Transit) (BART), a regional Rapid Transit system, connects San Francisco with the [East Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Bay_\(San_Francisco_Bay_Area\)) and San Jose through the underwater [Transbay Tube](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transbay_Tube). The line, which contains all except the Orange Line, runs under Market Street to [Civic Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civic_Center,_San_Francisco) where it turns south to the Mission District, the southern part of the city, and through northern [San Mateo County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Mateo_County,_California), to the [San Francisco International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_International_Airport), and [Millbrae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millbrae,_California).[^cite_note-transportation-387] BART also shares stations with SFMTA [Muni Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muni_Metro) under [Market Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_Street_\(San_Francisco\)).



[Muni Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muni_Metro), run by [SF Muni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Municipal_Railway)

Another commuter rail system, [Caltrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caltrain), runs from San Francisco along the [San Francisco Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Peninsula) to [San Jose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Jose,_California).[^cite_note-transportation-387] Historically, trains operated by [Southern Pacific Lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Pacific_Lines) ran from San Francisco to Los Angeles, via [Palo Alto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palo_Alto,_California) and [San Jose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Jose,_California).

[Amtrak Thruway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak_Thruway) runs a shuttle bus from three locations in San Francisco to its station across the bay in [Emeryville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emeryville,_California).[^cite_note-389] Additionally, BART offers connections to San Francisco from Amtrak's stations in Emeryville, Oakland and [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond,_California), and Caltrain offers connections in San Jose and [Santa Clara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Clara,_California). Thruway service also runs south to [San Luis Obispo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Luis_Obispo,_California) with connection to the [Pacific Surfliner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Surfliner).

San Francisco was an early adopter of [carsharing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carsharing) in America. The non-profit [City CarShare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_CarShare) opened in 2001[^cite_note-390] and [Zipcar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zipcar) closely followed.[^cite_note-391]



[Golden Gate Ferries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Gate_Ferry) connect the city to [North Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Bay_\(San_Francisco_Bay_Area\)) communities, while [San Francisco Bay Ferry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Bay_Ferry) connects the city to both the North and [East Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Bay).

[San Francisco Bay Ferry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Bay_Ferry) operates from the [Ferry Building](1774164.md) and [Pier 39](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pier_39) to points in [Oakland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oakland_Ferry_Terminal), [Alameda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alameda,_California), [Bay Farm Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_Farm_Island,_Alameda,_California), [South San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_San_Francisco_Ferry_Terminal), [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond_Ferry_Terminal), and north to [Vallejo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vallejo,_California) in [Solano County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solano_County,_California).[^cite_note-392] The [Golden Gate Ferry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Gate_Ferry) is the other ferry operator with service between San Francisco and [Marin County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marin_County).[^cite_note-393] [SolTrans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SolTrans) runs supplemental bus service between the Ferry Building and Vallejo.

To accommodate the large amount of San Francisco citizens who commute to the [Silicon Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_Valley) daily, employers like [Genentech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genentech), [Google](1092923.md), and [Apple](856.md) have begun to provide private bus transportation for their employees, from San Francisco locations. These buses have quickly become a heated topic of debate within the city, as [protesters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_tech_bus_protests) claim they block bus lanes and delay public buses.[^cite_note-394]

#### Freeways and roads

Further information: [List of streets in San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_streets_in_San_Francisco)



The [Bay Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco%E2%80%93Oakland_Bay_Bridge) connects the city to [Oakland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oakland,_California) and the East Bay.

In 2014, only 41.3% of residents commuted by driving alone or carpooling in private vehicles in San Francisco, a decline from 48.6% in 2000.[^cite_note-\:5-395] There are 1,088 miles of streets in San Francisco with 946 miles of these streets being surface streets, and 59 miles of freeways.[^cite_note-\:5-395] Due to its unique geography, and the [freeway revolts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highway_revolts_in_the_United_States#San_Francisco) of the late 1950s,[^cite_note-freeway-396] [I-80](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_80_in_California) begins at the approach to the [Bay Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco%E2%80%93Oakland_Bay_Bridge) and is the only direct automobile link to the East Bay. [US 101](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_101_in_California) connects to the western terminus of I-80 and provides access to the south of the city along San Francisco Bay toward [Silicon Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_Valley). Northward, the routing for US 101 uses arterial streets to connect to the [Golden Gate Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Gate_Bridge), the only direct automobile link to [Marin County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marin_County,_California) and the North Bay.

As part of the retrofitting of the Golden Gate Bridge and installation of a suicide barrier, starting in 2019 the railings on the west side of the pedestrian walkway were replaced with thinner, more flexible [slats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slat_\(aircraft\)) in order to improve the bridge's aerodynamic tolerance of high wind to 100 mph (161 km/h). Starting in June 2020, reports were received of a loud hum produced by the new railing slats, heard across the city when a strong west wind was blowing.[^cite_note-bridge_harmonic_resonance_issues-397]



[Lombard Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lombard_Street_\(San_Francisco\)) in [Russian Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Hill,_San_Francisco) is famed as "the most crooked street in the world".

[SR 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_Route_1) also enters San Francisco from the north via the Golden Gate Bridge and bisects the city as the [19th Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/19th_Avenue_\(San_Francisco\)) arterial thoroughfare, joining with [I-280](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_280_\(California\)) at the city's southern border. I-280 continues south from San Francisco, and also turns to the east along the southern edge of the city, terminating just south of the Bay Bridge in the [South of Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_of_Market_\(San_Francisco\)) neighborhood. After the [1989 Loma Prieta earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_Loma_Prieta_earthquake), city leaders demolished the [Embarcadero Freeway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_Route_480) and a portion of the [Central Freeway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Freeway), converting them into street-level boulevards.[^cite_note-freeway-396]

[SR 35](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_Route_35) enters the city from the south as [Skyline Boulevard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_Route_35) and terminates at its intersection of Highway 1. [SR 82](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_Route_82) enters San Francisco from the south as [Mission Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mission_Street), and terminates shortly thereafter at its intersection of I-280. The western terminus of the historic transcontinental [Lincoln Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Highway), the first road across America, is in San Francisco's [Lincoln Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Park_\(San_Francisco\)).

##### Vision Zero

In 2014, San Francisco committed to [Vision Zero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vision_Zero), with the goal of ending all traffic fatalities caused by motor vehicles within the city by 2024.[^cite_note-398] San Francisco's Vision Zero plan calls for investing in engineering, enforcement, and education, and focusing on dangerous intersections. In 2013, 25 people were killed by car and truck drivers while walking and biking in the city and 9 car drivers and passengers were killed in collisions. In 2019, 42 people were killed in traffic collisions in San Francisco.[^cite_note-399]

#### Airports

Main article: [San Francisco International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_International_Airport)



[San Francisco International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_International_Airport), one of the [busiest airports in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_busiest_airports_by_passenger_traffic)

Though located 13 miles (21 km) south of downtown in [unincorporated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unincorporated_area) [San Mateo County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Mateo_County,_California), [San Francisco International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_International_Airport) (SFO) is under the jurisdiction of the City and County of San Francisco. SFO is a hub for [United Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Airlines)[^cite_note-UAL-400] and [Alaska Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Airlines).[^cite_note-401] SFO is a major international gateway to Asia and Europe, with the largest international terminal in North America.[^cite_note-intlterminalfactsheet-402] In 2011, SFO was the eighth-busiest airport in the U.S. and the 22nd-busiest in the world, handling over 40.9 million passengers.[^cite_note-SFO-stats-2011-403]

Located in the [South Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Clara_Valley), the [San Jose International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Jose_International_Airport) (SJC) is the second-busiest airport in the Bay Area, followed by [Oakland San Francisco Bay Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oakland_San_Francisco_Bay_Airport), which is a popular, low-cost alternative to SFO.

#### Cycling and walking

Main article: [Cycling in San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cycling_in_San_Francisco)



A [Bay Wheels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_Wheels) station on [Market St.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_Street_\(San_Francisco\))

Cycling is a popular mode of transportation in San Francisco, with 75,000 residents commuting by bicycle each day.[^cite_note-2011_Bicycle_Count_Report-404] In recent years, the city has installed better [cycling infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cycling_infrastructure) such as [protected bike lanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protected_bike_lane) and parking racks.[^cite_note-405] [Bay Wheels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_Wheels), previously named Bay Area Bike Share at inception, launched in August 2013 with 700 bikes in downtown San Francisco, selected cities in the East Bay, and San Jose. The [San Francisco Municipal Transportation Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Municipal_Transportation_Agency) and [Bay Area Air Quality Management District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_Area_Air_Quality_Management_District) are responsible for the operation with management provided by [Motivate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motivate_\(company\)).[^cite_note-406] A major expansion started in 2017, along with a rebranding as Ford GoBike; the company received its current name in 2019.[^cite_note-407] Pedestrian traffic is also widespread. In 2021, [Walk Score](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walk_Score) ranked San Francisco the most walkable city in the United States.[^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410]

San Francisco has significantly higher rates of pedestrian and bicyclist traffic deaths than the United States on average. In 2013, 21 pedestrians were killed in vehicle collisions, the highest since 2001,[^cite_note-SF_Examiner_ped_safety-411] which is 2.5 deaths per 100,000 population – 70% higher than the national average of 1.5.[^cite_note-NHTSA_2012_Crashes-412]



A [San Francisco cycling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cycling_in_San_Francisco) event

[Cycling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cycling_in_San_Francisco) is becoming increasingly popular in the city. The 2010 [Municipal Transportation Agency (MTA)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Municipal_Transportation_Agency) annual bicycle count showed the number of cyclists at 33 locations had increased 58% from the 2006 baseline counts.[^cite_note-413] In 2008, the MTA estimated that about 128,000 trips were made by bicycle each day in the city, or 6% of total trips.[^cite_note-414]

As of 2019, 2.6% of the city's streets have protected bike lanes, with 28 miles of protected bike lanes in the city.[^cite_note-\:0-384] Since 2006, San Francisco has received a Bicycle Friendly Community status of "Gold" from the [League of American Bicyclists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_of_American_Bicyclists).[^cite_note-415] In 2022 a measure on the ballot passed to protect JFK drive in Golden Gate Park as a pedestrian and biking space with 59% of voters in favor.[^cite_note-416]

### Public safety

See also: [History of the San Francisco Police Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_San_Francisco_Police_Department)

The [San Francisco Police Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Police_Department) was founded in 1849.[^cite_note-417] The portions of the [Golden Gate National Recreation Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Gate_National_Recreation_Area) located within the city, including the [Presidio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidio_of_San_Francisco) and [Ocean Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ocean_Beach,_San_Francisco), are patrolled by the [United States Park Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Park_Police).

The [San Francisco Fire Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Fire_Department) provides both fire suppression and emergency medical services to the city.[^cite_note-418]

## Sister cities

Main articles: [Sister cities of San Francisco, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sister_cities_of_San_Francisco,_California) and [List of diplomatic missions in San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_in_San_Francisco)

San Francisco participates in the [Sister Cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sister_Cities_International) program.[^cite_note-419] A total of 41 consulates general and 23 honorary consulates have offices in the San Francisco Bay Area.[^cite_note-420]

In January 1980, Mayor [Dianne Feinstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dianne_Feinstein) signed a sister cities agreement with Shanghai during a visit to China.[^cite_note-\:05-421]
